I've updated layout A with a real pipeline, a collapsible sidebar and custom scrollbars. It's still a preview: nothing in the app has changed and nothing is committed. Open it at http://localhost:3000/layouts/workspace-sidebar.html.

## The pipeline
It now reads as stages that data flows through, not items in a list:
- **One connected line** runs through a node per stage. It fills in coral as stages complete, and it's dashed for the stages still ahead.
- **Phases** group the stages in order: Input (Records, Vendors) → Configure (Rules, Plan) → Execute (Run) → Review (Results).
- **Node states:**
  - completed stages are a filled coral circle with a check
  - the stage in progress has a coral ring (it pulses while a run is going)
  - the next stage is an empty circle
  - locked stages have a dashed outline and a lock
- **Each stage shows its output** in the mono font: `24 records mapped` → `3 rules on` → `1,344 pairs` → `judging 860 / 1,344`, with a mini progress bar while running.
- **A header** shows the evaluation name, a status line ("Run 2 running · 64%"), and a six-segment progress bar that animates while running.
- **The page you're viewing** is highlighted separately from how far the pipeline has got, and a locked stage can never be highlighted.

In the preview's top bar there's a **Setup / Running / Complete** switch, so you can watch the pipeline react to each moment.

## Collapse and expand
- The toggle sits at the start of the top bar, so it's always reachable, and the `[` key does the same. Your choice is remembered between visits.
- The sidebar animates between 264px and 68px. Labels fade and slide out, and the pipeline header and phase labels fold away smoothly instead of jumping.
- Collapsed, the pipeline keeps its nodes and connecting line, so you can still see progress. Hovering any icon or node shows a tooltip naming it.
- All animation is turned off for anyone who has reduced motion enabled.

## Scrollbars
They're thin, rounded and follow the theme in both light and dark. The sidebar's scrollbar only appears while your pointer is over it.

If this looks right, the next step is building it into the app:
- the app shell and sidebar
- the pipeline, driven by each evaluation's real state
- the shared spacing values on every screen

I'll delete the preview pages once that's done.

## My recommendation: stay with Phosphor, and start using its weights

Phosphor is already wired in through `src/components/ui/icons.tsx` (43 icons), and it's the strongest fit for what you're about to build. Here are the options worth considering:

| | **Phosphor** (current) | **Lucide** | **Tabler** | **Hugeicons** (free tier) | **Heroicons** |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Icons | ~1,500 | ~1,600 | ~5,900 | ~4,600 (free) | ~320 |
| Styles | **6 weights: thin, light, regular, bold, fill, duotone** | 1 (outline, adjustable stroke) | outline + ~1,000 filled | 1 free; the other styles are paid | outline, solid, mini |
| Feel | geometric, rounded | neutral, rounded | geometric, slightly technical | soft, modern | neutral |
| Licence | MIT | ISC | MIT | free tier with attribution terms; pro is paid | MIT |
| Ecosystem | good | **largest**; the shadcn default | large | growing | Tailwind's own |

**Why Phosphor wins here:**
1. **Empty states are where weights pay off.** An empty state wants a large, softer icon that carries the brand. Phosphor's **duotone** draws a second layer at partial opacity in the same colour, so a coral duotone icon gives a branded, illustration-like spot for free, with no separate illustration set. None of the others offer duotone, and Hugeicons charges for it.
2. **One family for every state.** Regular for interface icons, bold for small sizes, fill for active and selected states (a filled icon for the current pipeline stage, for example), duotone for empty states. Everything stays visually consistent because it's one family.
3. **It matches Poppins.** Both are geometric with round terminals, so icons and text look designed together. Lucide and Heroicons are more neutral; Tabler is a bit more technical.
4. **It's already integrated and costs nothing.** It's server-safe via its `/ssr` entry, only the icons you import are bundled, and no swap is needed.

**When I'd choose differently:**
- **Lucide**, if you want the most icons available and copy-paste compatibility with shadcn examples. The cost is one style only, so empty states would need separate illustrations to look rich.
- **Tabler**, if you expect to need a lot of specialised data and table icons. It has three times Phosphor's catalogue, but no duotone.
- **Hugeicons** looks the most "2026", but the styles that make it distinctive are paid, and its free licence has attribution terms.
- **Heroicons** is too small a set for this product.

**Switching cost:** the repo's icon rule routes every icon through one wrapper file. Swapping libraries means editing only that file and remapping the 43 icons, plus updating the rule and lint config. Choosing something other than Phosphor is a contained change either way.

If you go with Phosphor, the next step is to build three empty-state variants (for example: a large duotone icon only; a duotone icon in a soft coral circle with a background pattern; and a small composed scene from two or three icons) for you to compare side by side.

The inspiration page is ready at **http://localhost:3000/inspiration/index.html**. Use the tabs to switch directions, or **Compare all** to see each empty state across all four side by side; it works in Dark and Light. Every direction uses real Phosphor icons in the app's colours, on the same five real empty states:
- first visit, with no evaluations
- no records uploaded
- filters that match nothing
- results before the first run
- results that failed to load

Each direction also shows how it treats icons across the interface: sidebar, buttons, status chips, notices and table row actions.

| | Look | Borrowed from | Strongest at | Weakest at |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A · Quiet duotone** | Large duotone icon with a coral second layer, centred, nothing around it | Linear, Vercel | Calm, minimal | Can feel empty; closest to what you have now |
| **B · Icon tile** | Icons sit in soft rounded tiles; empty states are compact left-aligned blocks with a help link; section headings get tiles too | Stripe, GitHub | Feels like a working product; fits inside cards and tables, not just whole pages | Less memorable on its own |
| **C · Ghost UI** | A faint dashed preview of the list, table or tiles that will appear, with the message on top | Supabase, Airtable, Notion | Shows what's coming, ideal for data screens | Too heavy for errors and simple prompts |
| **D · Brand spotlight** | 64px coral duotone icon in concentric rings over a fading dot grid | Raycast, Arc | Most branded, illustration-like | Loud if used everywhere |

## My recommendation: B as the base, with C's ghost preview for table-shaped screens

1. **It fixes the "static page" feel at the source.** Tiles give icons a consistent home everywhere: empty states, section headings, notices. Icons become part of the structure rather than decoration floating next to text. A and D only change empty states, so the rest of the app keeps today's look.
2. **It fits a data tool.** Many of PairSight's empty states sit inside cards, tables and step pages, not on a blank page. B's compact left-aligned block looks right in a card; A's and D's big centred icons look stranded there.
3. **It's honest about the next step.** Every B empty state has one clear action and a help link. Errors switch to a red tile, so problems stand out without new patterns.
4. **C where a preview teaches something.** For results and the evaluations list, a ghost of the table shows what's coming. Use it only on screens shaped like a table or list, not for errors or upload prompts.
5. **D sparingly, if you want brand moments.** The spotlight is great for the one first-visit screen; across the app it would be too much.

Since this is all interim until your redesign, B is also the cheapest to carry forward: tiles and weights are a few component rules, easy to restyle later.

Nothing is committed. The page is a temporary file in `public/inspiration/`, and I'll delete it once you've picked a direction or a mix.

Every key from your screenshots exists with the new text, all 262 message lookups in the app resolve, and production builds render everything correctly.

**Fixed in code:** `src/i18n/request.ts` now loads messages through a static import per locale instead of a template-string import. The template form is the pattern Turbopack doesn't reliably track, and it's what made messages go stale four times this session.

**One thing I can't do without asking:** restart your dev server. The stuck instance needs a restart with its compile cache cleared, and the server is yours, so I didn't want to kill it from under you. Either:

The gap check is done. M1 and M2 already built most of M3. What's left is two acceptance criteria that no test proves yet, a few correctness risks, and UX-plan features.

### Not proven against M3's acceptance criteria
1. **"Retry succeeds" is never shown.** The stand-in Jev service's only failure is permanent, so the e2e checks that the pair fails *again*. It needs a mode that fails once and then succeeds, and a test that Incomplete drops to 0.
2. **"Recomputes without calling Jev" is never checked.** The threshold change works (scores are reused), but nothing counts calls before and after.

### Correctness risks
These are the agent's findings; I'll confirm each before changing anything.
3. **Stale claims can overlap.** A pair can take up to about 64 s to judge (3 attempts × 20 s timeout plus backoff), but it's treated as stale after 60 s. Claiming a batch also isn't atomic, so two tabs could judge the same pair.
4. **Starting a new run changes earlier runs.** It resets earlier failed assessments to pending, which contradicts "completed runs stay reproducible".
5. **Failures show raw codes and aren't grouped.** They appear as codes like `http_422`, and the Run page doesn't group failures by reason.

### Missing UX-plan features
6. **Results table:** no PairSight decision column; the low-confidence flag is squeezed into the score cell.
7. **Per-pair Retry** in pair detail.
8. **Threshold popover:** a slider, the score distribution chart and a preview of how counts change. This needs a chart library (Recharts) plus Popover and Slider components.
9. **Results header:** a run selector and a run status chip.
10. **Run page:** elapsed time, and announcements as the run progresses.
11. **Keyboard on Results:** up/down, Enter, and `/` to focus search.
12. **Loading skeletons.**
13. **Smaller items:** the "How this works" popover, scroll position kept on Back, an "evaluated" status filter, the "86% evaluated" wording, and the per-vendor chart (first on the cut line).

**My recommendation:** build 1–7 first. That completes M3's acceptance, fixes the risks that could corrupt data or mislead, and adds the decision column and per-pair retry, with little visual surface for your redesign to redo. Then 8–9: the threshold popover is the biggest single piece and the only one adding a dependency, and it shapes the Results page you'll redesign, so it may be worth doing it in your design directly. Leave 10–13 until after the redesign, or cut them.

Which scope do you want? I'll start once you say.

I've built items 1–7 on `feat/run-and-results`. All 10 PairSight e2e tests pass against a production build. Lint, typecheck, format, the secret check and the build pass, and unit tests are 207/217 (the same 10 Windows-only failures). Nothing is committed.

1. **A retry can now succeed, and the test proves it.** The stand-in Jev service has a pair that fails its first call and succeeds when retried. The failures test now walks through it:
   - After the run, Incomplete is 2 and failures show as "Request rejected".
   - Retrying that pair from its page gives a score, so Incomplete drops to 1 and Both matched rises to 2.
   - Retry failed on the Run page makes exactly one new call to Jev, and the permanently failing pair stays incomplete.
2. **Threshold changes are proven not to call Jev.** The stand-in counts calls per upload (each test upload gets its own email domain), and the test checks the count is unchanged after Apply.
3. **Pair claims are safe.**
   - Each pair is now claimed one at a time, just before it's judged, and only one caller can win the claim, so two tabs can't judge the same pair.
   - The claim is refreshed on every Jev attempt. A live claim now goes at most about 23 s without being touched, well inside the 60 s limit.
   - The new code for judging pairs is shared by the run loop and the single-pair retry.
4. **Earlier runs:** judgments are shared between runs by design, and a successful score is never changed. Only failed pairs get judged again, and every run that includes the pair then shows the new score. I wrote this down in `docs/PairSight-V1-Scope.md` under "Runs and reuse". I also fixed a real gap: if a later run reset an earlier run's failed pairs and was then abandoned, the earlier run was stuck with waiting pairs. Its Run page now offers Resume.
5. **Failure reasons are readable.** Raw codes like `http_422` become labels like "Request rejected", "Rate limited" or "Timed out"; the raw code stays in the tooltip. The Run page lists "Why pairs failed" with a count per reason. The mapping is in `src/lib/pairsight/failure-reasons.ts`, with unit tests.
6. **Results table:** a PairSight decision column now sits beside the vendor's ("Not judged" for incomplete pairs), and low confidence has its own Flags column.
7. **Per-pair Retry** is on the pair page for failed pairs. It judges the pair immediately and shows the score, or says it failed again. If the API key is the problem, the run stops and the Run page offers Resume, as in the main run loop.

**Not tested end to end yet:** the new Resume for an earlier run left with waiting pairs, and the claim timing. Both need a run that's interrupted midway, which the current tests can't easily produce.

**Changed files:**
- **Engine:** `run-engine.ts`, `assess.ts`, `runs.ts`, the run actions
- **UI:** the run progress and action bar, the pairs table, the status chip, the Jev panel, the new `retry-pair-button.tsx`
- **Tests:** `scripts/jev-stub.mjs`, the failure fixtures, `e2e/pairsight.spec.ts`
- **Text:** `messages/en.json`, the V1 scope doc

Since nothing was pushed, I undid those local commits, kept every change, and redid them, stopping at the first failure. The final commits contain only their own files and the tree is clean.

When you run the full e2e: PairSight's tests need the stand-in Jev environment, so start a production build on a spare port as I did, rather than reusing your dev server on 3000:
```
TYPESAFE_API_BASE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:4010/v1 TYPESAFE_API_KEY=e2e-stub-key pnpm exec next start -p 3100
E2E_BASE_URL=http://localhost:3100 E2E_PAIRSIGHT=1 pnpm test:e2e
```
Afterwards, the test rows the run adds to the local AI spend table can be deleted with the `delete from ai_usage …` command used earlier.

The configuration-error test is committed (`a801396`), followed by the session log (`01c5a3b`). Nothing is pushed.

## Where M3 stands

Everything the delivery plan lists for M3 is built, and all three of its acceptance criteria are proven by e2e tests against the stand-in Jev service:
- **A forced failure stays incomplete and a retry succeeds:** a single-pair retry judges the pair, Retry failed makes exactly one new Jev call, and the permanent failure stays incomplete.
- **A threshold change makes no Jev calls:** the stand-in counts calls, and the count doesn't change on Apply.
- **A disagreement can be inspected,** from the keyboard as well.

Retries, reclaiming abandoned pairs, the configuration error, resume, reuse across runs, the threshold control, the dashboard and the filters are all in. The local gates and the full e2e suite (13 tests) pass on Windows.

## Not done or not verified yet
1. **CI hasn't seen M3.** The branch is unpushed, with no PR. Locally, 10 unit tests and 7 standards checks fail only because of Windows, so Linux CI is the real confirmation of those. This is the next concrete step whenever you want it.
2. **No live run with the real key since M1.** Every M3 path (retries, the configuration error, single-pair retry) has only run against the stand-in. The plan's manual check is a live run on fictional data, plus confirming no record content reaches logs or the AI spend table. You hold the key, so that run is yours. Run it locally, and don't paste the key here.
3. **Not covered by tests:**
   - reclaiming a pair abandoned mid-call (worth testing directly, since it's where pairs could get judged twice);
   - Resume on an earlier run left with waiting pairs;
   - the loading skeletons and screen-reader announcements (a manual check);
   - from before: the theme menu, sign-out and the error pages.

   None of these block anything, but the first is the one I'd test.

## Decisions to confirm
1. **Changing the threshold rewrites the run's stored threshold.** Applying 0.40 updates that run (and the evaluation's default), so the run no longer remembers it was created at 0.50. The schema marks this field as editable after the run, and only decisions change, never scores, so the design allows it. If "reproducible" should also mean "shows its original threshold", we'd store that separately.
2. **Runs share judgments.** A later retry can fill in an earlier run's failed pairs. This is now written down in the V1 scope doc; please confirm that's the behaviour you want.
3. **Retry exists only on the pair page.** The UX plan also mentions retrying from incomplete rows in the results table. Retry failed on the Run page covers the bulk case, so I left the row button out.

## Bigger-picture flags
- **Timing:** the delivery plan's Friday target is **tomorrow (2026-10-02)**, and its gate is M4: the fictional demo dataset, a prepared snapshot, `pnpm demo:seed`, the public `/demo` page, a minimal landing page and the manual checklist. That's the only milestone left before the gate, and the plan never cuts the demo. Your UI redesign doesn't block M4, but they'll compete for time before Friday.
- **Still open in the scope doc:** TypeSafe's rate limit and request size are undocumented. Neither is a blocker, because the engine retries 429s and each request carries one pair.

## Suggested order
1. Push `feat/run-and-results` and open the M3 PR, so Linux CI runs.
2. Run the live check while CI runs.
3. Merge, then start M4 (`feat/demo`).

Optionally, add the abandoned-pair test before step 1. Tell me which of these you want.

I've explored the whole Magic Patterns app: all 127 files of source, read in parallel by three agents, plus a visual pass through the live preview of the landing page, demo results (both tabs), a pair, and sign-up. Nothing in the repo was changed.

## What the design is
It's a 13-screen React app running on mock data:
- landing, sign-in/up, not-found;
- a public `/demo` with its own header and a "Fictional data" bar;
- the evaluations dashboard;
- Records, Vendors, Rules, Plan and Run;
- Results with Overview and Detailed analysis tabs;
- pair detail.

The colour tokens, Poppins and JetBrains Mono, and Phosphor icons are the same as ours.

## How far it is from what we have
**The look (big, but mechanical):**
- **Shell:** a 248/60px sidebar, 48px bars, and the page scrolls inside the main area instead of the whole window. The pipeline uses 20px steps with a black check when done, a coral ring with the number when active, a "Next" tag, and locked steps with reason tooltips.
- **Building blocks:** a lighter card style with 10px corners and no icon tiles, plus segmented controls, chips, a general dialog and info tips.
- **Type:** raw 13/12/11px sizes rather than named roles.
- **New dependencies:** Recharts (3 charts), framer-motion (page fades, animated numbers, staggered rows), sonner toasts and date-fns.
- **New brand mark:** two rounded rectangles in #E45742.

Our IconTile and ghost empty states don't exist in the design.

**New features, most computable from data we already store:**
- **Results:**
  - Precision, Recall and F1 per vendor, labelled "AI-estimated · evaluated pairs only" and "Provisional";
  - coverage, including "possible pairs not compared";
  - a decision matrix and a threshold-sensitivity chart;
  - vendor comparison and "where vendors agree";
  - field patterns with written insights, top disagreements;
  - a score histogram split by the vendor's decision, findings by rule;
  - a review workload panel.
- **Evaluations dashboard:** KPIs, latest results with P/R/F1, "Continue setup" with stage progress, search, rename and delete.
- **Setup:**
  - field-by-field mapping with sample values;
  - live validation with inline fixes ("Assign reference", "Remove row");
  - a per-vendor editor with drafts and rename;
  - a live summary on Rules;
  - a "records to pairs" ledger with "Turn off" buttons on Plan;
  - an idle "Ready to run" page with Pause and Resume.
- **Pair page:** token-level differences, a "not sent to Jev" badge, and a field summary.

**Needs real backend and schema work:**
- **Human review:** a new table plus actions, then "Your review" (Same individual / Different individuals / Unsure plus a note), an "AI reference ↔ Human-reviewed" toggle, a review filter and column, and progress.
- **Export:** findings or summary CSV.
- **Public demo:** a seed, a public read path, and threshold and review changes that last only for the session.
- **Pause:** a paused run state.
- **Vendor details:** stored stats (duplicates, self-pairs, unknown references), plus group-format vendor files.
- **Rules:** derived rule fields (first initial, birth year, postcode district, email domain).
- **Records:** file size on uploads.

Our scope docs mark several of these as "Next": metrics, human review, export, group format and a polished landing. Copying the design brings them into scope now.

## Conflicts to settle when we plan
Places where the design, being a mock, contradicts how our product works:
1. **Runs:** one run per evaluation with id-less `/run` and `/results` URLs, versus our real runs with ids, a run switcher and a starting threshold.
2. **Unknown vendor references:** the design ignores them, but the V1 scope says block.
3. **A column mapped twice:** the design warns, we block.
4. **Changing the mapping after save:** the design allows it; our saved records are fixed.
5. **Country:** full names including "Mixed / international", versus our 2-letter code, which phone normalization needs.
6. **Stage gating:** explicit "confirm" steps versus ours derived from data.
7. **Jev fields:** at least 2 fields and an optional "Does not count", versus our 1 and required.
8. **Default threshold:** 0.60 versus our 0.50.
9. **Demo:** editable for the session, versus read-only in the UX plan.
10. **Category markers:** square dots, versus our distinct shapes for colour-blind safety.
11. **Prototype limits:** 100 pairs, buckets of 10 and 2 vendors are prototype values. I'd keep our 5,000, 50 and 3, and lay out three vendors in the design's style.
12. **Greeting:** "Good morning, {name}" needs a display name we don't store.
13. **Things only we have** and the design drops: elapsed time, failure reasons grouped by type, per-row Retry, the cost estimate, the starting threshold and real run statuses. Do they stay?

This is a much bigger job than a reskin; it amounts to most of the remaining product, M4's demo and landing included. In plan mode I'd propose a phased order: foundation and shell first, then setup screens, Results with metrics, human review and export, and finally demo, landing and auth. I'll bring these conflicts as questions, and won't decide them silently.

The finding notes are saved for the planning turn. I closed the browser tab I used. Ready for plan mode when you are.

Phase 0, the design-system foundation, is built. All 13 e2e tests pass; typecheck, lint, the build and the unit tests (the same 10 Windows-only failures) are clean. Nothing is committed.

**What changed:**
- **Tokens:** our colours already matched the design exactly, so these are additions:
  - the series colours for charts, now covered by the contrast test;
  - the elevated shadow;
  - a 10px `rounded-md`;
  - the fixed logo coral;
  - MP's base styles: 14px body text, one 2px focus ring with an offset, tabular figures in mono numbers.
- **Type:** two new roles, `type-ui` at 13px for controls, menus and table text, and `type-caption` at 11px. Page titles are now 22px, rising to 24px on wider screens. DESIGN.md is updated.
- **Motion:** MP's fade-in, pop-in and float-in animations with its easing curve, plus the `motion` library (the current name for framer-motion).
- **Charts:** Recharts 3, plus `react-is` 19. React 19 needs that version for Recharts to recognise its own components; without it, charts would quietly fail to draw.
- **Restyled in place**, with call sites unchanged:
  - Button gains MP's radius and press effect, plus `quiet`, `icon` and `icon-sm`. It now defaults to `type="button"`; every form already passes `type="submit"` where it needs to.
  - Checkbox, switch, input, textarea and skeleton.
  - Menus, popovers and tooltips: elevated surface, border, 13px text, no tooltip arrow.
  - Toasts: restyled to MP's look, as you chose.
- **New primitives:** Chip, Panel, Segmented, Dialog, InfoTip, Kbd, NativeSelect, ViewToggle, Reveal and AnimatedNumber.
- **Icons and logo:** 21 more icons plus a bold weight, and the new mark (two rounded rectangles) with a 15px wordmark.

Screens look only slightly different so far: buttons, fields, menus and the logo. The new layouts arrive with Phase 1 (the shell) onwards, and the older components are removed as each screen is ported.

**Two things to flag:**
- **Temporary excludes:** TypeScript and ESLint were picking up the Magic Patterns folder, so I added a clearly marked exclude to `tsconfig.json` and `eslint.config.mjs`. Like your `.gitignore` line, both come out when the folder is deleted. I suggest leaving all three uncommitted.
- **Recharts version:** I used 3.x, because 2.x is marked deprecated; the components MP uses are the same in both.

Do you want to review Phase 0 first, or should I continue with Phase 1, the shell (sidebar, pipeline, top bar, page headers and action bar)?

The progress bar isn't broken. Your Run 3 had nothing to animate, but a run that really calls Jev would still look jumpy.

**What happened with Run 3:** it made no Jev calls. The database shows 0 pairs judged during the run and all 19 reused from an earlier run with the same pairs and fields. The run was already at 19/19 when the page opened and finished on its first update, 0.7 seconds after it started.

**How the bar moves on a real run:** each update judges a batch of 10 pairs, 4 at a time, and the page only gets new numbers when the whole batch is done. So the bar:
- jumps in steps of 10 pairs, with a quick 150 ms slide between steps;
- sits still while each batch is being judged, which with real Jev can be several seconds;
- never shows anything above 0 under "Running", because the page only hears back after a batch has finished.

A brand-new evaluation of about 20 pairs would show roughly two jumps.

I can make it feel live with three small changes:
1. **Smaller batches:** judge 4 pairs per update (the same number Jev handles at once), so the bar updates every 4 pairs instead of every 10.
2. **Slower slide:** about 600 ms per step instead of 150 ms, so the bar glides rather than snaps.
3. **"Working" shimmer:** a subtle moving stripe on the bar while the run is active, so it's clearly busy between updates. This isn't in Magic Patterns, which fakes progress one pair at a time.

Getting a live "Running" count would mean checking progress in the background while a batch runs. That's more work, and I'd leave it out.

Should I make changes 1–3 as part of Phase 3 (or pick which), before committing and moving on to Phase 4?

All three are fixed and checked in the browser, and the e2e suite still passes (15 of 15). Nothing is committed yet.

1. **Continue setup empty state:** it's now centred: a check icon in a circle, "All caught up", the line "Every evaluation has its results. Start a new one when you're ready.", and the New evaluation button underneath.

2. **Dialog jumping from top-left to centre:** this was a CSS clash affecting every dialog in the app. The dialog is centred with one setting, and its opening animation used a second setting that added the same offset again. For the 200 ms of the animation the dialog was shifted left and up by half its own size, then snapped to the centre when the animation ended. The animation now uses the same setting as the centring. I measured it frozen on its first frame: it now starts at the centre, 4 px low (the intended small rise), and settles exactly in the middle.

3. **"Enter a name" flashing on close:** clicking Cancel, the ✕ or outside first takes focus away from the field, and the form checked the name at that moment. The new evaluation, rename and onboarding (`/welcome`) forms all did this. Now none of them check when you leave the field; errors appear only when you press Create / Save / Continue, then update as you type. In the browser, Cancel and ✕ close cleanly, and submitting an empty name still shows the error.

**Your session:** while checking in Chrome, the tab switched to your own account. Logins are shared across localhost ports, so your sign-in on :3000 replaced my test session. Nothing in your account changed: I only opened that dialog and closed it, or submitted it empty, which saves nothing. I finished the checks in an isolated test browser instead.

The clean-up after tests now deletes AI usage rows only for test accounts (`e2e+…@pairsight.test`). Before, it deleted everything from the last few minutes, which could have included your real rows. I saved both points to memory so future sessions follow them.

**Before saving** (empty, then with a file chosen):
- **Dropzone:** Magic Patterns' centred design with "Browse files". The Vendors screen shares this component, so it updated too.
- **File bar:** the file name, "Not saved yet · CSV", rows, columns and size, and a Replace file button.

  - "Use all suggestions" and a one-click "Use {column}" fill in the suggested columns.
  - Picking a column that another field already has moves it to the new field, so one column never has two roles (#10).
  - A note lists the columns you haven't mapped.

Replace file first asks "Replace records file?" (Keep current file / Replace file). After that, the uploader shows with "Keep current file" to back out.

**How it works:**
- So the panel can update instantly, the file preview now checks every column as a possible reference once, without sending the file again on each change.

The error happens because this file is a different kind of vendor export from the one PairSight reads today. Nothing is broken.

**What the file looks like.** I checked your `Downloads/pairsight_vendor_match_results.csv`. It's a **group** export: one row per *record*, with records that belong together sharing a `PairId`.

```
PairId  RecordType  Record  …  Account Code
1       Matched     1          CUST-001
1       Matched     2          CUST-002   ← PairId 1 = CUST-001 + CUST-002
2       Matched     3          CUST-003
2       Matched     4          CUST-004   ← PairId 2 = CUST-003 + CUST-004
```

**What PairSight reads today.** Only the **pair** format: one row per *matched pair*, with both record references on the same row.

```
record_a   record_b
CUST-001   CUST-002
CUST-003   CUST-004
```

**Why it asks for a first and second record column.** In a pair file, those are the two columns holding the two halves of each pair. Vendors name them differently (`record_a`/`record_b`, `id_1`/`id_2`…), so you pick them, and the form pre-selects its best guess.

Your file has no such pair of columns. The guess was `PairId` and `RecordType`, so PairSight looked up `RecordType` values as record references. "Matched" isn't a reference in your source records, so all 12 rows failed: "12 references aren't in the source records: … 'Matched'".

**Why group files aren't supported yet.** This was planned. The V1 scope says the pair CSV comes first and "a standard group CSV comes next if time permits". A group of *n* records means all n(n−1)/2 pairs, de-duplicated, with large expansions flagged before a run. Magic Patterns' Vendors screen also shows only the pair format, so the group format was never built.

Two ways forward:

1. **Add group-format support as part of the Vendors rebuild (Phase 5, next).** I'd recommend this. The Vendors screen would get a format choice: *one pair per row* or *one record per row, grouped*.
   - For a group file you'd pick the **group column** (`PairId`) and the **record reference column** (`Account Code` or `Record`, whichever matches your source file's reference). The first/second columns wouldn't appear.
   - PairSight would expand each group into its pairs, de-duplicate them, and show what it found before you add the vendor.
   - Your file would then load as-is.
2. **Convert this file to pair format for now** and add group support later.

Either way, the form should recognise a file like this and say "this looks like one record per row; choose the group format", instead of showing 12 cryptic reference errors. I'd add that in both cases.

Do you want group-format support built into the Vendors phase? If so, I'll include it alongside the Vendors questions (#12 drafts/rename, #13 unknown references) before I start.

Vendors is rebuilt. You name the vendor and drop the file, and PairSight works out the rest; it only asks about columns when the file is genuinely unclear. All 16 e2e tests pass, and lint, typecheck, format and 241 unit tests are clean (the other 10 are the usual Windows-only standards self-tests). Nothing is committed yet; it sits on top of the Records, locked-route and home-route changes.

**Your file now loads as-is.** I checked it in a test browser: it's read as group format, grouped by `PairId`, references in `Account Code`, giving 6 pairs from 6 groups with nothing unknown, without picking a column.

**How the format is worked out:**
- PairSight compares every column's values with the references in your records file. Two columns holding references means a pair file. One reference column, plus a column whose values repeat across rows, means a group file.
- A column named like a group (PairId, cluster_id, match_id…) is preferred over one that only happens to repeat, like a state or a score. That's why your constant RecordType and per-pair Score didn't confuse it.
- A pair file with a few typos still reads as pairs if its columns are named as two sides (`left_id`/`right_id`, `record_a`/`record_b`…); the bad references then show as rows to fix.
- When it's genuinely unclear, a short message says why and the format and column pickers open with the best guess filled in. "Change" opens them at any time while the file is loaded.

**The screen** follows Magic Patterns:
- **Layout:** a vendor list on the left showing "Pair format · ready" or "Needs: upload the export", with Add vendor below. The editor on the right has the vendor name (saved when you leave the field) and Remove vendor, then the matching results.
- **Matching results:** the file row with Replace, the "Read as …" line, a stats strip (matched pairs and how many groups, duplicates removed, self-pairs skipped, unknown references), any problems, and the completeness checkbox.
- **Drafts:** each vendor is saved as a draft as soon as you add it, so you can leave and come back. Ticking "This export contains all of Vendor A's matches…" makes it ready. Continue to rules stays blocked with "Finish Vendor B: upload the export" until every vendor is ready. Group exports also expand to every pair within each group, each pair counted once, with expansions past the 5,000-pair cap blocked.
- **Unknown references** still block, with their row numbers, as you chose.

**Under the hood:**
- **Separate drafts table:** drafts live in a new `vendor_drafts` table, so plans, runs and results can never see a half-finished vendor. The pipeline keeps Vendors "in progress" (it shows "1 vendor in progress") while one exists.
- **Migrations:** two new ones, applied to your local database: the table plus a stats column on finished vendors, then a separate RLS migration as the rules require. The RLS check passes on all 15 tables.

**Limitations to know about:**
- **Finished vendors:** you can rename or remove one, but not swap its file or untick it. To change the file, remove the vendor and add it again. Magic Patterns allows both; doing that safely means turning a vendor back into a draft, which I left out for now.
- **Vendors added before this change** have no stored stats, so they show their file name without the stats strip.
- **Plan page:** it doesn't yet mention an unfinished draft (drafts aren't included in runs). I'll add that note when I port the Plan screen.

Fixed at the source. Reload the Plan page and it shows the right copy; your dev server picked up the fix without a restart.

**Root cause:** the translations file (`messages/en.json`) was imported into the code, so Turbopack compiled it into the server bundle like any other module. What a page showed then depended on Turbopack noticing every edit to that file and rebuilding it. Sometimes it didn't: it missed a change, or restored an old copy from its on-disk cache after a restart. The new code then asked for keys the stale copy didn't have, and next-intl showed the raw key names (`Plan.ledgerTitle`, `Plan.back`…). It always hit the screen whose copy had changed most recently, which is why it kept moving between modules.

**How I confirmed it, on your running dev server:**
- Your server was the dev server, started at 3:18, and its output is separate from my production builds, so those weren't clobbering it.
- I edited one public string (the sign-in title). The file changed straight away, but the server kept serving the old text for about 10 seconds.
- Your Plan copy was a change it missed outright. It only caught up when my probe touched the file again.

**What was there before:** a comment in `src/i18n/request.ts` records an earlier fix for this exact symptom (switching to a static import). That only made it rarer; the file was still part of the bundle.

**The fix:** translations no longer go through the bundler. `src/i18n/request.ts` reads them from disk on the server instead:
- **In development**, every request reads what's on disk. On your server, three edits in a row each showed on the very next request.
- **In production**, each file is read once and kept, since deployed files don't change.
- **`next.config.ts`** tells the build to ship the `messages/` folder with the app, since nothing imports it any more. I checked that the build includes it for every page.
- **The locale-to-file table** is still typed, so adding a language without its file fails typecheck. A new unit test checks every messages file exists and is valid JSON.

**Checks:** all 16 e2e tests pass on the production build; lint, typecheck and format are clean, and 244 unit tests pass (the other 10 are the usual Windows-only standards self-tests).

This is uncommitted, alongside the Plan redesign. Next I'll run local-review and commit both (this fix as its own commit), then start Phase 6, unless you'd like to check something first.

Here's the full list since M3 started on `feat/run-and-results`. It covers 34 feature/fix commits plus session logs, all local and nothing pushed. The Phase 6 work at the end is done but not yet committed.

## Part 1: Finishing M3 (run and results)

**Run engine and reliability**
- Pairs are claimed atomically and the claim is renewed on each attempt, so two tabs or a retried request never judge the same pair twice.
- An earlier run left with waiting pairs can be resumed.
- A rejected or missing API key stops the run with the fix spelled out ("The run stopped"), and Resume continues once the key works.
- The run page shows elapsed time and announces progress milestones to screen readers.

**Failures and retries**
- Failure reasons are grouped by type ("Why pairs failed").
- You can retry a single failed pair from its pair page, or from its row in the results table.
- Failed pairs stay "Incomplete" and never count as "no match".

**Results**
- The results dashboard is complete: category tiles, coverage, filters, pagination and a run switcher.
- Results can be filtered by assessment status, and each URL filter is read on its own, so one bad value doesn't break the rest.
- The PairSight decision and flags show as table columns.
- Each run keeps its starting threshold ("Run started at 0.50").
- Changing the threshold re-decides from saved scores without calling Jev again, and a new run reuses existing judgments.
- Loading skeletons for the run, results and pair pages.

**Smaller items**
- The decision rules moved to `src/lib` so client and server share them.
- The vendor name suggestion updates after a vendor is added.
- New popover and slider primitives.

**E2E tests** added for retries, threshold changes, the results dashboard, the run page, two vendors, a rejected API key, the starting threshold and row retry.

## Part 2: UI revamp to the Magic Patterns design

**Phase 0: design system**
- New design tokens (colour series for charts, shadow, corner radius, the brand colour), type sizes, three animations, and a focus ring that applies everywhere.
- Restyled building blocks: button variants, checkbox, switch, input, textarea, skeleton, dropdown menus, tooltips, popovers and toasts (our own, not sonner).
- New building blocks: Chip, Panel, Kbd, Segmented control, Dialog, InfoTip, ViewToggle, Reveal and AnimatedNumber.
- 21 new icons, plus the new logo glyph and wordmark.
- Added `motion`, `recharts` and `react-is`.

**Phase 1: app shell**
- New sidebar (wide/collapsed, plus a phone drawer), with a numbered pipeline that explains why a step is locked.
- Top bar with breadcrumbs and a status chip, plus theme and account menus.
- The page scrolls inside the main area, and the action bar spans the full width.

**Onboarding**
- A required full-name step at `/welcome` (up to 100 characters).
- The account menu shows your name and initials.
- Adds a `User.name` column (database migration).

**Phase 2: results and pair detail**
- Results Overview tab:
  - Headline metrics and coverage.
  - Pairs by category, and the decision matrix.
  - Threshold sensitivity, vendor comparison and vendor overlap.
  - Field patterns and top disagreements.
  - Score distribution, source breakdown and workload.
- Detailed tab:
  - Live filters, including a new score filter, and a sortable table.
  - A page-size choice (all pairs, 50 per page by default, as you chose).
  - Highlights the pair you came back from.
- Threshold popover with a precision/recall preview, Reset to the starting value, and a confirmation toast.
- Pair detail:
  - Side-by-side field comparison with differences highlighted, "not sent" and "format differs" markers, and a show-all-columns toggle.
  - "How this pair got here", the restyled Jev panel and each vendor's decision.
- The metrics are computed on the server, with unit tests.

**Phase 3: run page**
- A state card: "Assessing pairs…" / "Run complete" / "Stopped".
- Progress shown as "3 / 3", counts with coloured dots, and an "N incomplete" chip.
- Retry sits next to the failure reasons, and the stop alert lists numbered fix steps.
- The action bar has a Back button, which every screen can now use.

**Every dropdown is themed**: the browser's own select lists are gone, the setup screens' selects were fixed too, and the old native select was removed.

**Phase 4: evaluations dashboard**
- A greeting by the viewer's own clock, a summary strip, and latest results with precision/recall/F1 per vendor.
- Continue setup with stage bars, and an all-evaluations table with search and a row menu.
- New evaluation, Rename and Delete dialogs; names must be unique per account, ignoring case.
- A first-visit empty state with a product preview, and a centred "All caught up" panel.
- The rules for each stage's status now live in one place, shared by the sidebar and the dashboard, with tests.

**Phase 5: setup screens**
- **Records:**
  - Map each PairSight field to one of your columns, with Suggested tags and sample values.
  - A Validation panel that checks references as you change the mapping.
  - A preview with problem rows marked.
  - The saved view is read-only, and replacing the file asks first.
  - Continue saves and goes straight to Vendors.
- **Vendors:**
  - The format (pair or group) and the columns are worked out automatically from your records' references, with Change to override.
  - **Group-format files are supported** (each group expands into its pairs).
  - Vendors are saved as **drafts** you can come back to (new `vendor_drafts` table + RLS migration), can be renamed, and show stats.
  - A completeness confirmation makes a vendor ready; unknown references still block.
- **Rules:**
  - Autosaves as you go, with a live summary on the right.
  - The country is chosen from a list of names (stored as the 2-letter code).
  - Jev needs at least 2 fields.
  - An "e.g." example key and Buckets/Pairs/Largest columns per rule.
  - Composite rules can now be edited.
  - The unsaved-changes warning was removed, since there's nothing left unsaved.
- **Plan:**
  - A "From records to pairs" ledger with the cost estimate.
  - "What Jev sees" and "Every vendor-matched pair is included".
  - An over-limit alert with a per-rule **Turn off** button.
  - A notice when a vendor draft is unfinished.

**Phase 6: landing page, sign-in, sign-up and not-found** (done, not committed yet)
- A new landing page and an auth layout shared with onboarding.
- Field-by-field errors on submit, and a show/hide password toggle.
- A centred not-found page whose links depend on whether you're signed in; the general error screens share it.

## Fixes found along the way
- **Missing translations shown as raw keys** (the root cause): the copy was bundled by Turbopack, which sometimes missed or cached edits. It's now read from disk on every request in dev.
- **Locked steps reachable by URL**: they now redirect to the step the evaluation is up to.
- **Signed-in users sent to `/`**: they now go to `/evaluations`. `/`, `/sign-in` and `/sign-up` redirect them, and the logos and not-found links point there.
- **Dialogs jumped from the top-left to the centre** as they opened (a CSS clash).
- **"Enter a name" flashed** when closing a dialog; forms now check only on submit.
- **Open redirect**: the sign-in return address (`next`) could be pointed at another site; that's now refused.
- **Rules stuck on "Saving…"** when a preset needed a column that isn't mapped.

## Tests and checks
- E2E grew from 13 to 17 tests, with several rewritten for the new screens.
- 15 test files added, covering metrics, the results overview, stage rules, profile and safe-next redirects, the records mapping, vendor detection and group expansion, per-column CSV checks, sample keys, the plan's two-field minimum and the messages files.
- `/local-review` ran before every commit batch.

Next is the Phase 7 clean-up, starting with committing Phase 6.

Here's M4 as `docs/PairSight-Delivery-Plan.md` defines it, checked against the code on `main`:

| M4 item | Status |
|---|---|
| Fictional dataset | Mostly there. `e2e/fixtures/demo-source.csv` (24 people) with Vendor A and Vendor B files covers the plan's demo scenario, but it's only been used for e2e so far. |
| **Prepared snapshot** | **Not done.** It needs a real Jev run on that dataset, saved with its model id and date. |
| **Idempotent `pnpm demo:seed`** | **Not done.** No script yet. The database is ready for it: evaluations have a `demo` type with no owner, and runs can be marked "prepared". |
| **Public `/demo`** | **Not done.** Results and pair detail, read-only, viewable without signing in, with a banner like "Prepared results recorded from Jev \<model\> on \<date\> — fictional data". |
| Minimal landing | Done, and more than minimal. It needs its "Try demo" buttons back once `/demo` exists. |
| E2E with a Jev stub | Done (17 tests). Only the plan's "plus the public demo" test is missing. |
| **Manual checklist** | **Not done.** The live flow with the real key on fictional data, and a check that no record content reaches logs, analytics or `ai_usage`. |

**Already done from the "Next" roadmap:** multiple vendors (up to 3), group CSV, deleting an evaluation, precision/recall/F1, and the polished landing. Still deferred: human review, CSV export, analytics events.

So the real M4 work is one feature, the demo, built as:

1. **Real snapshot:** run the 24-person dataset through real Jev once, check every category is filled (tweak the fixtures if not), and record the model and date.
2. **`pnpm demo:seed`:** loads that snapshot as an owner-less demo evaluation. Running it twice gives the same result.
3. **`/demo`:** the existing Results and pair pages in read-only mode for signed-out visitors. The threshold can still move without saving; nothing else is editable or calls Jev.
4. **Turn the "Try demo" buttons back on** on the landing page, sign-in and the empty dashboard.
5. **A demo e2e test** and the manual checklist, then the M4 PR.

Before I plan it in detail, two things I need from you:
- **Real Jev key:** step 1 needs a run with your actual TypeSafe key on that fictional data. It costs real but tiny money: about 30 pairs. Are you OK with that, and is the key set in your `.env.local`? Don't paste it here.

From creating an evaluation to the F1 scores, every number comes from what the user uploads, the real Jev judgments on those pairs, and real calculations. The sandbox is the only seeded data, and its own banner says so.

The phrase isn't about seeded data. It's a data-handling caution taken from `docs/PairSight-V1-Scope.md` ("Personal data"): no real identifiable customer data should go to Jev until TypeSafe's data handling and our authorization are in place. The records' fields really are sent to TypeSafe and stored in our database, so it's warning users not to upload real customer records yet. It shows in three places:
- **Landing hero note:** "The sandbox needs no account. Use fictional or anonymized records for your own."
- **Landing footer:** "Fictional or anonymized data only"
- **Plan side panel**, just before Start evaluation: "Use fictional or anonymized records only for now."

Read on its own, it sounds like the app runs on fake data. Which way to go depends on whether that data-handling question is settled:
- **Still open:** keep the caution but say what it means. For example, on Plan: "The fields you chose are sent to Jev. Until data handling is agreed, don't upload real customer data." I'd drop the footer version, which is the most confusing one.
- **Settled:** remove all three, and update the V1 Scope note.

You're not missing much. Most of what you're asking about is your decision, not something I need. Here's where the line came from and what it does and doesn't cover.

**Where it came from:** `docs/PairSight-V1-Scope.md`, written during planning, says no real identifiable customer data goes to Jev "until TypeSafe's data handling and our authorization are established". That was a placeholder for questions nobody had answered yet:
- **What TypeSafe does with the data:** whether it keeps the records, for how long, and whether they're used to train models.
- **Whether there's an agreement covering that:** a data-processing agreement between whoever runs PairSight and TypeSafe.

I can't answer either from the code or the API docs.

**What telling the user covers, and what it doesn't.** You're right that it's a real disclosure: the user knows an AI judges their records. The gap is that the records usually aren't the user's own data. They're their customers' names, emails and phone numbers. The uploader agreeing to send them to Jev isn't the same as those people agreeing. Under rules like GDPR, the uploader's company normally needs a lawful basis, plus a processing agreement covering everyone who touches the data: PairSight, and TypeSafe as a subprocessor. That's what the planning doc meant by "authorization". It's a legal and business question, and I'm not the right one to rule on it.

**What this means in practice:** PairSight only runs on localhost today, with no production database. So right now the line mostly reads as "this app uses fake data", which is wrong and confusing, as you noticed. It only starts to matter when real companies upload real customer lists.

My recommendation is to replace the caution with a plain disclosure and settle the agreement question before a public launch:
- **Plan** (just before Start evaluation): "Jev, TypeSafe's AI, judges each pair. The fields you choose are sent to it."
- **Landing:** "The sandbox needs no account." Drop the footer line.
- **V1 Scope doc:** change the "Personal data" note to an open item: "Before real customer data: confirm TypeSafe's retention and processing terms."

Should I make those changes? Or, if you already have that agreement with TypeSafe (or it's the same company), I can drop the note from the doc entirely.

## Landing (`/`), the big one
Reload the page to see the sequence:
1. **Header** (0 s): slides down softly. It's now sticky: clear over the hero, then frosted with a hairline border once you scroll.
2. **New pill above the title** (0.1 s): "Vendor matching, checked pair by pair", with a small coral dot that pulses gently.
3. **Headline** (0.16–0.7 s): each word rises out of a blur, one after another.
4. **Gradient on the title:** the ending, "or missed the right ones.", fades in with a coral-to-amber gradient that slowly shifts. In light mode the gradient runs deeper red to burnt orange so it stays readable.
5. **Description, buttons, then note** (about 0.7–0.85 s): they follow in that order.
6. **Product preview** (from 0.45 s): it tilts up from below into place. Its numbers, category tiles and rows then appear one by one, and the score bars fill from the left. Once it's settled it floats very slightly, up and down over 7 s.
7. **Background:** three soft brand-colour glows drift slowly behind the hero, over a faint dot grid that fades out at the edges.

9. **Scrolling down:** "How it works", its three steps (in order, with the connector lines drawing across), the "Honest about…" rows and the closing call to action all arrive as they scroll into view. Both are done. "How it works" now plays as you scroll to it, and the Results animations run slower so you can watch them. All 20 e2e tests pass, and lint, typecheck and formatting are clean. Nothing is committed.

## 1. Landing: sections play when you scroll to them
The earlier version tied the animation to scroll position, so it only moved while you scrolled and was finished almost as soon as the section appeared. That's why it looked static. Now each section stays hidden until you scroll it into view, then plays a timed sequence once.

**"How it works":**
- The heading rises first.
- Then, every 0.6 s, a step plays:
  - its number pops in with a small bounce and a ring that pulses out once;
  - its title and description rise a beat later;
  - the connecting line draws across to the next number, which pops just as the line reaches it.
- The full sequence takes about 2 seconds.

**Other sections:**
- **"Honest about what it measures":** the intro, then the three rows, one after another.
- **Closing call to action:** the line, then the buttons.

**When it plays** (checked on a desktop window and a phone-sized one):
- If a section only peeks above the fold when the page loads, it waits for you to scroll it up.
- The bottom call to action at the very end of the page still plays.
- A fast flick past a section on a phone still plays it, so it's never left invisible.
- It works the same in every browser.

## 2. Results page: slower so you can see it build
| What | Before | Now |
|---|---|---|
| Charts (threshold sensitivity, score distribution, vendor comparison) | 0.3 s | 1 s, starting 0.25 s after their card lands |
| Cards arriving | 0.24 s, 40 ms apart | 0.5 s, 70 ms apart, with a slightly bigger lift |
| Precision, recall, F1 and counts counting up | 0.9 s | 1.2 s |
| Bars: metric tracks, category tiles, coverage, sources, vendor overlap | appeared instantly | wipe in from the left over about 1.1 s |

The count-up and card changes also apply to the dashboard, since they share those components.

One side effect: charts that change with the threshold (for example after you click Apply) now take a second to redraw. If that feels slow, I can keep the slow first draw and make threshold changes quick again.

Run `pnpm dev` to try it; your dev server on :3000 isn't running.

Items 1–5 and 10 are done on `feat/m4-signoff`, and the full e2e suite passes (36 of 36). Nothing is committed.

## 1–3. Design review gate
**Screenshot sweep.** I captured 42 screens and states (every step, error states, failed and stopped runs, the sandbox, auth pages, menus), each in light and dark at 1440, 1024 and 390 px. Three reviewers went through them and found about 40 issues; I checked the main ones against the screenshots, fixed them, and re-ran the sweep to confirm:
- **1024 px windows looked broken.** Layouts switched to multiple columns based on window width, but the sidebar leaves the page only about 780 px. Results, Records, Rules and the dashboard were squeezed, overlapping or cut off. Page layouts now follow the page's own width, so a 1024 px window gets a clean single column. The layout at 1280 px and wider is unchanged.
- **The Plan ledger didn't add up** (6 + 2 − 6 ≠ 8). It now reads rule matches + vendor pairs − duplicates = unique pairs. Rules whose fields aren't mapped are no longer counted as "on" in the Plan, the sidebar or the summary.
- **Runs:**
  - A run with failed pairs showed a green "Run complete". It now says "Finished with 2 incomplete pairs", and "Retry failed" is the main button.
  - While a run is going, the sidebar, status chip and breadcrumb now update instead of showing "Ready to run".
  - A stopped run no longer says "in progress", and the duplicated sentence is gone.
  - The back button says "Plan", and "leaving pauses processing" only shows while the run is actually running.
- **Toasts** covered the Continue button. They now sit above the action bar.
- **Phones:**
  - The pair's AI verdict and the records' validation now come first.
  - The breadcrumb keeps the current page visible.
  - The sandbox banner is one line.
  - Keyboard hints are hidden on touch screens.
- **Truncated text now wraps:** results workload, sources, run picker, vendor filter, Continue setup and the chart's "1.0" label. Textareas grow with their text.
- **Tables that scroll sideways** now fade at the edge that has more to show.
- **Copy and numbers:**
  - Plurals fixed ("1 vendor pairs", "1 assessed pairs").
  - Zero cases fixed ("The other 0 are added anyway").
  - "5460" is now "5,460".
  - The empty-filter hint now mentions search.
- **Colours:**
  - Orange meant two things on one page; the score distribution now uses neutral colours.
  - The "confirmed" vendor card is green instead of error-red.
  - "Suggested" pills are blue instead of error-red.
- **Sandbox:** the Plan page now has a "See the run" button.

**Accessibility.** I added `@axe-core/playwright`, a test-only dependency. axe found four structural problems, now fixed: the read-only buttons' labels, the Plan ledger's list markup, and a scrollable table keyboard users couldn't reach. It found no contrast problems in either theme.
- Automated checks now cover every public and sandbox screen, in both themes.
- I scanned the signed-in screens (all steps, dialogs, the threshold popover) with axe in both themes by hand: clean.
- In a keyboard-only pass, every tab stop shows a focus ring. The charts had none; that's fixed.

## 4. New e2e tests
- **`e2e/public.spec.ts`** (runs in CI too): the theme choice surviving a reload with no flash, the 404 page, the `/en` redirect, and axe on the public pages.
- **PairSight spec:** sign-out, a 404 for a missing or invalid evaluation id, and three vendors.
- **Sandbox spec:** axe on every sandbox screen in both themes.

## 5. Third vendor

I like the direction, with one correction to the framing and a different answer on limiting.

**The framing: the threshold doesn't change how strict Jev is.** Jev never sees the threshold. It scores each pair the same way whatever the setting, and the threshold is only where PairSight draws the line on that score: 0.82 counts as a match at 0.50, not at 0.90. That matters for the copy. If the setting is labelled as Jev's strictness, users will think a stricter setting makes Jev look harder. A label like "How sure Jev must be for a pair to count as a match" (Lenient / Balanced / Strict) says what it does.

**What I'd agree with: decide the threshold before seeing results.** Right now someone can open Results and slide the threshold until their preferred vendor wins. Making it a setup choice turns each run into an honest, fixed claim: "at this strictness, Vendor A's precision is X". I'd do it like this:
- **Rules:** a "Match threshold" section next to the identity definitions. It's part of "what counts as the same individual". It offers three presets (Lenient 0.35 / Balanced 0.50 / Strict 0.70) plus a custom value, and autosaves like the rest of the page.
- **Plan:** shows the chosen threshold in the "What Jev sees" panel, and the run takes it on Start.
- **Results:** the threshold becomes read-only for that run. The sensitivity chart and the popover's "what if" preview stay, but there's no Apply. A link says "Change it in Rules and run again".

**Limiting changes: I wouldn't add a limit.** Changing the threshold never calls Jev, because scores are saved, and a new run reuses every score already judged. So a threshold-only re-run costs nothing and finishes in seconds. The abuse you're worried about is quietly rewriting the result. The fix is to make each change visible rather than rare: one threshold per run, kept forever, so each change becomes a new numbered run in the run switcher. That's an audit trail instead of an arbitrary cap like "three changes", which would frustrate honest users without stopping anything.

**Cost of building it:**
- **No migration:** the evaluation already stores a threshold, and so does each run.
- **Changes:** the threshold field and its copy on Rules, showing it on Plan, removing Apply and its server action from Results, and updating tests and docs. The sandbox already works this way, so it needs nothing.
- **One snag:** the Run page shows a run as "assessing" even when every score is reused. A threshold-only re-run would flash past, so it may need a small "nothing new to assess" state.

It's a behaviour change, not a fix. My suggestion is a small PR of its own after #7 merges, so #7 stays the sign-off. If you'd rather have it on #7 to save the extra merge, that works too.

If you're happy with the shape, two things to settle: are the preset values right (0.35 / 0.50 / 0.70), and should custom values be allowed or just the three presets? Given the live check (different pairs scored 0.11 or lower, same pairs 0.76 or higher), all three would sort the clear cases the same way; they only differ on the borderline ones.

The threshold now lives in Rules: three presets plus a custom value. It's built and checked, but not committed. I'm holding it for you to try first; your dev server on :3000 is running with it.

**What changed**
- **Rules:** a new "Match threshold" section between the identity definitions and the candidate rules.
  - It offers Lenient 0.35, Balanced 0.50 (default), Strict 0.70, or Custom, which takes any value from 0.01 to 0.99.
  - It autosaves like the rest of the page, and the server enforces the same 0.01–0.99 limits.
  - The copy says what the threshold actually does: "How sure Jev must be for a pair to count as a PairSight match. Jev scores every pair the same way; this only sets where the line is drawn."
  - A small 0–1 bar shows where the line sits, with a short note for the chosen preset.
- **Plan:** a "Match threshold" card at the top of the side panel shows the value and its preset name (e.g. "0.50 Balanced"), plus a Change link back to Rules. It notes that the run keeps it.
- **Results:** the threshold is fixed for the run.
  - The popover still previews other values live (slider, distribution, precision and recall), and Reset returns to the run's own value.
  - Apply is gone, replaced by "Change in Rules".
  - The Apply server action and its update function are removed, so a run's threshold can't change after the run starts.
- **Runs:** a new run now takes its threshold from Rules. Before, it inherited the previous run's.
- **Re-runs:**
  - A run that only changes the threshold reuses every saved score and is created already complete, with no "Assessing…" flash and no new Jev calls.
  - Each change becomes a new numbered run in the run switcher, so earlier results stay as they were.
- **Sandbox:** the threshold is visible in Rules but disabled, like the rest of that page. Its "Sign up to save a threshold" button is gone, since there's no Apply left to block.
- **No migration:** both the evaluation and each run already store a threshold.

**Tests and docs**
- **E2E:** the threshold test is rewritten for the new flow:
  - the Results preview has no Apply, and its link goes to Rules
  - on Rules, a custom value, then Lenient
  - the Plan shows "0.35 Lenient", with 8 scores reused and 0 new
  - Run 2 is complete at once and results re-decide with no new Jev calls
  - Run 1 still decides at 0.50
- **Sandbox spec:** checks there's no Apply and the threshold radios are disabled.
- **Unit test:** a new one for preset names and limits.
- **Docs:** the UX plan, V1 scope and delivery plan describe the new flow.

**Checks**
- **E2E:** all 36 pass. The first two full runs each had 2 timeouts, a different pair of tests each time. Each failing test passed when run alone, and all 36 pass with two parallel workers. The machine was busy: your dev server recompiles on every file I edit. CI runs on its own machine, so it won't hit this.
- **Gates:** lint, typecheck, format, env, secrets and RLS pass, plus 251 unit tests, with the same 10 failures as before that only happen on Windows.

Have a look on :3000: Rules → Plan → Start, then Results and its popover. Once you're happy, I'll commit it in groups and push it to PR #7.

The Runs centre is built, including your mid-way notes on animation and empty states. All 36 e2e tests pass. Nothing new is committed: the threshold work is committed locally on `feat/m4-signoff`, this sits on top uncommitted, and nothing is pushed.

**Pipeline:** now Records → Vendors → Rules → Plan → Results; Run is no longer a step. While a run is going, Results is the active step and fills in. Old `/run` and `/runs/<id>` links redirect to that run's Results with the Runs centre open on it.

**Runs centre:**
- **Opening it:** a **Runs** button in the top bar on every app page, including the dashboard and sandbox. It shows a spinner and a count while something is running.
- **Contents:** every run across your evaluations, in two tabs. Active is the default while something runs, otherwise Completed.
- **Completed holds:**
  - finished runs
  - runs finished with failures, with "Retry N failed" on the card
  - stopped runs, which have a red card with the reason ("TypeSafe rejected the API key"), the fix, and Resume
- **Card front:** run number and evaluation, status, live progress while running (otherwise pairs and threshold), and the one action that matters.
- **Card Details:** threshold and preset, judgments (reused vs new vs failed), why pairs failed, Jev model, vendors and their files, rules, fields sent, country, identity text, times and the run ID.

**Starting a run:** Start opens the centre on Active with live progress. When it finishes, the run moves to Completed and you're taken to its Results, with the centre still open over them. If you close the centre early, the run keeps going and a toast offers "View results" when it's done.

**What keeps it running:** the loop that drives a run now lives in the app shell instead of the old Run page. So a run continues on any page, and only closing the app pauses it.

**Motion and empty states (your note):**
- Dialogs now ease out as well as in, slightly quicker on close.
- Card details ease open and closed instead of popping.
- Cards arrive in a short stagger, replayed when you switch tabs.
- The dialog keeps a steady height between loading, empty and filled.
- Empty tabs get an icon, a title and one line: an hourglass and "Nothing running right now" for Active, a tick and "No finished runs yet" for Completed.

**Two bugs found and fixed while testing:**
- **Stale cards:** after a pair was retried on Results, the Runs centre kept showing the run's old counts. The progress it had seen live was overriding fresher data from the server; now the newer one always wins, and opening the centre always refetches.
- **Build failure:** the React Compiler rejected one function being used before it was declared.

**Checks:**
- Lint, typecheck, format, env, secrets and RLS all pass.
- Unit tests: 251 pass. The 8 stage tests are updated for the five-step pipeline, and the same 10 Windows-only failures remain.
- E2E: 36 of 36, run with two workers because your dev server is busy recompiling.
- No schema change; I only updated a comment, and Prisma confirms there's no drift.

Your dev server on :3000 should pick this up. Try starting a run from a Plan, closing the centre mid-run, a stopped run (the e2e stub can reject the key), and the sandbox's Runs button. Once you're happy, I'll commit it and push everything to PR #7 in one go.

I'm on the latest `main` (`c3109e2`, PR #7). I re-read the product overview, V1 scope, delivery plan, UX plan and README, plus what we decided along the way. This is everything still open; nothing has been started.

## A. In the product plan, not built yet
These are the "Next" items. Their controls are hidden in the UI until they're built.
1. **Human review:** on a pair, mark it **same / different / unsure**. Human decisions are stored separately from Jev's, and the comparison updates where they apply. You set this aside.
2. **Exports:** a CSV of findings, and a comparison summary that records the identity definition, scope, threshold and how much was human-reviewed. You said we'd discuss this later.
3. **Analytics events:** PostHog is set up, but none of PairSight's screens send events. You set this aside.

## B. Deferred by the plan
4. **Background worker:** runs pause when the app is closed and resume when it's reopened.
5. **Fuzzy blocking and derived rule fields** (e.g. first initial, email domain). UX decision #16 says "Not now".
6. **More input formats:** vendor-specific export adapters, and XLSX uploads.
7. **Measurement depth:** sampling the pairs your rules exclude, and verified ground-truth labels for true accuracy.
8. **Scale and scope:**
   - People only, no companies or products.
   - Limits of 500 records, 5,000 pairs per run, 3 vendors and 40 columns.
9. **Live deployment:** on-premises, with Jev on the customer's own infrastructure.

## C. Open questions about Jev (V1 scope, "Still open")
10. **Rate limit:** undocumented, and never hit at 4 requests at a time. The engine already backs off and retries if it is.
11. **Request size limit:** also undocumented. It isn't a concern at one pair per request.

## D. Not in the plan, but gaps a real user would hit (your call)
12. **Account management:** there's no "forgot password", no way to change name, email or password, and no way to delete an account.
13. **"The uncertain category":** the V1 scope lists it under "Later". Today borderline scores are only a low-confidence flag and filter, not a category. Do you still want a category, or is the flag enough?

## E. Repo and housekeeping
14. **Template pages still shipped:**
    - `/admin`, the template's staff console with role-based access.
    - `/analytics-demo`, a template demo page.

CSV export is built on a shared file connector, and all 36 e2e tests pass. There's a new **Export CSV** button on Results; it's on `feat/polish`, not committed yet.

**The connector (`src/server/pairsight/tabular/`)**
- Every file PairSight reads (records, vendor exports) and writes (findings) now goes through one place. Each format is an adapter that turns bytes into a grid and back; the checks (empty file, header problems, row and column limits, ragged rows) are shared, so every format behaves the same.
- **Uploads now send the file itself instead of its text.** That was needed for Excel, which is binary. The supported formats and the 1 MB limit are now defined once and shared by the drop zone, the browser check and the server.
- **Adding Excel later** is one new adapter file plus a library, and listing `.xlsx` in the format list. Import and export both get it. Nothing else changes.

**The export**
- **Where:** an "Export CSV" button in the Results header, next to the run picker. It downloads `pairsight-<evaluation>-run-<n>-findings.csv`.
- **Contents:** one row per pair in the run:
  - both records' references and each mapped field side by side
  - Jev's status, score, model, time and failure reason
  - PairSight's decision at the run's threshold, and the low-confidence flag
  - what found the pair (rule names, or "Added by a vendor")
  - for every vendor, its decision and category

  Categories match the Results tiles exactly.
- **Excel-friendly:** the file is UTF-8 with a marker Excel uses to show accented names correctly. Any cell starting with `=`, `+`, `-` or `@` is written so a spreadsheet won't run it as a formula.
- **Access:** you can export your own runs; the sandbox's run can be exported without an account. Anyone else's run gets a 404. Downloads are never cached, and the log records only the run id and row count, never record contents.

**Tests**
- **Unit:** 258 pass (7 new), with the same 10 Windows-only failures. They cover the connector's writing (quotes, line breaks, BOM, the formula guard), write-then-read round trips, the column limit and an unsupported file type, plus the findings table's columns, categories, failed pairs and low confidence.
- **E2E:** every upload test passes through the new file transport. The demo flow now downloads the CSV and checks its 8 rows and categories, the sandbox download works signed out, and someone else's run gets a 404.
- **Checks:** lint, typecheck, format, env, secrets and RLS pass.

**Docs:** the UX plan has an "Export CSV" entry, the roadmap moves the CSV export to Built (with the PDF summary and Excel import/export as Next), and the README notes the connector.

One thing I can't check from here: please open an exported file in Excel and confirm the names display correctly and nothing runs as a formula. Then tell me whether to commit it to `feat/polish`.

Excel import and export now run on the same shared connector as CSV. Every gate passes and all 37 e2e tests pass, including a new Excel round-trip test. Nothing is committed yet; that waits for you to test it.

**What to test**
- **Records upload:** drop a `.xlsx`. If the workbook has more than one sheet with data, a "Choose a sheet" picker lists each sheet with its row count. After you pick one, the file bar says "Not saved yet · Excel · Sheet Customers". Dates come through as `1990-04-12`, numbers like `94110` as typed, and formulas as their value.
- **Vendor upload:** works the same way, picker included. The file line ends with "· Excel · Sheet …".
- **Export on Results:** the button is now an **Export** menu with "CSV (.csv)" and "Excel (.xlsx)", and it works signed out in the sandbox too. The Excel file has one "Findings" sheet with a bold header that stays in place when you scroll and has filter arrows. Columns are sized to fit, the Jev score is a real number, and every other cell is plain text.
- **Errors:** a corrupt or password-protected workbook gets a clear "couldn't read this workbook" message. An old `.xls` gets "Choose a .csv or .xlsx file".

**Where it differs from the plan**
- **Reading:** I dropped ExcelJS's streaming reader. It misses the sheet list when that comes last in the file, which is how ExcelJS itself writes workbooks, so sheet names and text cells broke. It now loads the whole workbook. To keep that safe, it first checks how large the file says it unpacks to and refuses anything over 30 MB, so a small upload can't expand into a huge one.
- **Hidden sheets:** they're skipped, and sheets are listed in the order of the workbook's tabs.
- **Test files:** instead of committing `.xlsx` fixtures, the e2e tests build the workbooks from the existing CSV fixtures (`e2e/workbooks.ts`), so the two formats can't drift apart.
- **Sandbox test:** it now checks the Excel export, and the main demo test still checks the CSV one.
- **Not covered end to end:** a group-format vendor file as `.xlsx`. The unit tests cover reading Excel files, and group detection doesn't care which format the file came in.

**Checks run:** lint, typecheck, format, env and secret checks, the production build, and unit tests (264 pass; the same 10 `check-standards` tests fail on Windows as before, unrelated to this work). The connector has 10 tests, covering cell conversion, the sheet choice, the row limit, a corrupt file, the size guard and the round trip. I also cleaned up the e2e accounts' `ai_usage` rows.

I updated the UX plan, the roadmap (Excel moves to Built), the Product Overview and the README.

The main new file is `src/server/pairsight/tabular/xlsx.ts`. The UI pieces are `sheet-picker.tsx`, `file-type.tsx` and `results/export-menu.tsx`.

When you've tested it, tell me if you want this committed. I'd make two commits: the CSV export with the connector, then Excel.
