Here's everything since the last devlog list (1 Oct, 22:54). That list ended with the Phase 6 landing redesign done but not yet committed, so this picks up from there.

## 1 Oct: closing out M3 (PR #5, merged)
- Committed Phase 6: the landing, sign-in and not-found page redesign.
- **Phase 7 clean-up:**
  - Removed the Magic Patterns leftovers and retired components: IconTile, EmptyState, Badge (replaced by Chip), Section, and two old Button variants.
  - Deleted five unused shadcn components, five unused type styles, the duotone icons, and 37 unused translation keys.
  - Updated `DESIGN.md`, the component and icon rules, and the UX plan's "What shipped" section, which records how all 20 design differences were settled.
- Opened PR #5 ("finish M3 and rebuild the UI to the final design") after a full e2e run (17/17). You merged it.

## 2 Oct: M4, the sandbox (PR #6, merged)
- **Demo became a sandbox:** `/sandbox` lets visitors walk the whole app read-only with no login, on one fictional evaluation (24 records, a pair-format and a group-format vendor).
- **Real Jev snapshot:** recorded once with your key; `pnpm demo:snapshot` and `pnpm demo:seed` load it, and running the seed twice gives the same result.
- **Access:** a new `EvaluationScope` controls who can read an evaluation (the owner, or anyone for the sandbox). Every change still requires the owner.
- **One set of screens:** each page became a shared screen used by both `/evaluations/[id]` and `/sandbox`, and links are built from a base path.
- **Read-only:** save, upload and run controls show a "Sign up to …" tip. There's a sandbox banner, the sidebar shows Sign up / Sign in when signed out, and the landing, auth pages and empty dashboard link to it.
- **Opening an evaluation:** it now goes to wherever the evaluation is up to. Results opens only once the latest run is complete; otherwise you land on the active step.
- **Copy:** the "fictional or anonymized data only" warnings are gone, replaced by a plain "Jev, TypeSafe's AI, judges each pair". The V1 scope now records the on-premises stance.
- **Motion everywhere:**
  - Landing: staged hero entrance, gradient title, tilting product preview, drifting glows, and sections that play when scrolled into view.
  - App: sections cascade in on each page, counters count up, score bars fill.
  - Results charts and cards slowed down so you can watch them build.
  - Reduced motion is respected.
- **Fixes:** the cards in the vendor and rules columns were touching; horizontal scroll on most pages (the action bar was a scrollbar too wide); phone overflow; a hidden label escaping a table; the pair page's vendor list wrapping; F1 running into the next number on the dashboard.
- 21/21 e2e passing.

## 2 Oct: M4 sign-off (PR #7, merged)
- **Design review:** 42 screens and states captured in light and dark at 1440, 1024 and 390 px. Fixes from it:
  - Layouts now respond to the room the page actually has (container queries), so 1024 px windows no longer break.
  - The Plan's pair count now adds up (rule matches + vendor pairs − duplicates = unique pairs).
  - Run states tell the truth: "Finished with N incomplete pairs", correct stopped and in-progress states.
  - Toasts no longer cover the action bar, and phone layouts were reordered.
  - Text wraps instead of truncating; plurals, zero cases and number formatting fixed; colours and the score distribution corrected.
- **Accessibility:** added axe checks on every public and sandbox screen in both themes, fixed what they found, and did a keyboard-only pass.
- **Your decisions:** the focus ring is now blue (`--focus`, a GitHub-style ring), and the status chip says "Ready to plan".
- **Minor fixes:** emails wrap at sensible points, the vendor step's wording, the phone action bar layout, and the dialog placeholder.
- **New e2e:** `public.spec.ts` (theme survives reload with no flash, 404, axe), sign-out, an invalid evaluation id, and three vendors (new Vendor C fixture).
- **Template:** `example.prisma` renamed to `user.prisma` (no migration), and the smoke test removed.
- **Settled values:** the low-confidence band stays ±0.1 and the default threshold 0.50, recorded in the docs.
- **Threshold in Rules:**
  - Lenient 0.35, Balanced 0.50 or Strict 0.70, plus a custom value from 0.01 to 0.99.
  - Plan shows the chosen threshold, and each run keeps its own.
  - On Results it's preview-only (no Apply).
  - A threshold-only re-run reuses every score, so it finishes instantly with no Jev calls.
- **Runs centre:**
  - Run is no longer a pipeline step; the five steps are Records → Vendors → Rules → Plan → Results.
  - A Runs dialog on every page has Active and Completed tabs. Stopped runs show in Completed with a failed look, the reason and Resume.
  - Each card's details list threshold, judgments, model, vendors, rules and timing.
  - Animated cards and dialogs, and proper empty states.
  - The run loop moved into the app shell, so a run continues on any page.
- 36/36 e2e passing.

## 2 Oct: `feat/polish` (5 commits, not pushed)
- Removed the template's `/admin` and `/analytics-demo` pages.
- Docs rewritten to describe what v1 shipped: Product Overview, V1 Scope, the roadmap (Built / Next / Deferred) and the README.
- **Landing:** parallax illustrated "How it works" cards, a new "A closer look" section, and a coral gradient call-to-action panel.
- **Glass UI style** beside Classic, chosen under Theme → Style. You kept the subtle version, and Classic stays the default.

## 2 Oct: exports and Excel (`feat/polish`, uncommitted, what you're testing now)
- **Shared tabular connector** (`src/server/pairsight/tabular/`): every import and export goes through it. Each format is one adapter, and the checks are shared. Uploads now send the file itself rather than its text.
- **Findings export:** one row per pair with the mapped fields, Jev's status, score and model, PairSight's decision, the low-confidence flag, what found the pair, and each vendor's decision and category. It works in the sandbox signed out, someone else's run gets a 404, and downloads are never cached.
- **CSV:** UTF-8 with a BOM, and cells that would run as formulas are made inert.
- **Excel import:** `.xlsx` for records and vendor files. Dates, numbers and formulas are read as you see them. A workbook with several sheets of data shows a sheet picker, and the file bar names the sheet read. Corrupt or oversized workbooks are refused safely.
- **Excel export:** an Export menu (CSV / Excel). The workbook has a bold, frozen, filterable header, sized columns, and the score as a number.
- 37/37 e2e passing (a new Excel round-trip test), 264 unit tests passing.

The PDF summary report is built: the Export menu now has a third item, "Summary report (.pdf)", alongside CSV and Excel. All 37 e2e tests pass and 270 unit tests pass (the 10 failures are the usual Windows-only standards self-tests). Nothing is committed.

The sandbox's report is 5 A4 pages:
1. **Verdict and scorecard:** "On these 24 records, Clearline Resolve matched most like Jev's judgment (F1 0.50)". It also says the result holds at every threshold, so it doesn't depend on where the threshold was set. When different vendors are best on wrong merges or on missed duplicates, a "Fewest wrong merges: …" or "Fewest missed duplicates: …" line names them. The scorecard ranks every vendor with matched pairs, agreed with Jev, wrong merges, missed duplicates, precision, recall and F1, each explained in plain business terms.
2. **Tradeoffs:**
   - What each vendor gets wrong, and which fields those pairs usually share or differ on.
   - A chart of each vendor's F1 at every threshold.
   - A head-to-head sentence, e.g. "Where only one of Northwind Match and Clearline Resolve matched a pair, Jev sided with Clearline Resolve in 3 of 5." It's built for the analyst justifying a choice to their boss.
3. **Examples (pages 3–4):** each vendor's clearest wrong merges and missed duplicates. Each one shows the names, Jev's score, how the pair was found, and every field side by side marked same, different or missing.
4. **Method and scope:** the files, the fields sent to Jev, the identity definition, rules and coverage. It includes "268 possible pairs were never compared", the Jev model, run times, scores reused versus judged in this run, "Human review: none", and a glossary.

Every page has a footer, and the caveat that these figures are AI-estimated agreement with Jev, not verified accuracy, is on the first page. It works signed out in the sandbox, and anyone else's run gets a 404.

Two things differ from the plan:
- **Font:** the app gets Poppins from Google at build time, so there's no font file to embed. The PDF uses the built-in Helvetica, so nothing has to be bundled and it renders the same in every PDF reader.
- **One rendering quirk:** react-pdf drops page numbers when line height is set on the page, so it's set on each text style instead. There's a comment on it in `styles.ts`.

Coverage:
- **Unit tests:** 6 new ones in `report-summary.test.mts` cover ranking, the safest and most complete vendors, whether the lead holds across thresholds, head-to-head, examples and field comparisons.
- **E2E:** the demo flow downloads and reads the PDF and checks that a signed-out request gets a 404. The sandbox test checks both vendors' ranking and the head-to-head line.
- **Visual check:** I've only looked at the sandbox's two-vendor report. The one- and three-vendor reports are covered by text checks in e2e but I haven't looked at them as PDFs; worth downloading one from your own runs.

**To review:** open the sandbox, go to Results → Export → Summary report. The copy I reviewed is at `C:\Users\onais\AppData\Local\Temp\claude\C--t1-naisxx\90a4606b-2381-42a5-9032-5e4e39778137\scratchpad\sandbox-summary.pdf`. Your dev server on :3000 will pick it up after a restart, since there are two new dependencies (`@react-pdf/renderer`, and `pdf-parse` for tests only).

The UX plan, roadmap (PDF moved to Built), V1 scope, Product Overview, README and `DESIGN.md` are updated. After you've reviewed it, I'll run local-review and e2e-review again and commit in feature groups.

I fixed the page 3 overlap: example cards that reach the bottom of a page now move whole to the next page instead of piling onto each other.

**Cause:** react-pdf lays out a "keep together" block wrongly at a page break when it sits inside other wrappers. The cards were inside a section and a vendor wrapper, so they collapsed at the bottom of the page. My previous change made it more likely.

**Fix:** in `src/server/pairsight/report-pdf/`, every example card and its heading now sit directly on the page with no wrappers. The section heading is part of its first card, so it moves with it and can't be left alone at the bottom.

**Checked:** I couldn't download a fresh copy from the app because Docker Desktop isn't running, so the database is down. Organisations and products are built on `feat/entity-types-and-field-dictionary`. The full e2e suite passes 40 of 40 on a fresh build (including new organisation, product and sandbox-switch tests). Unit tests pass apart from the usual 10 Windows-only failures, and lint, typecheck, format, env and secrets are clean. Nothing is committed yet.

**Live Jev check (14 calls, fictional data):** the 0.50 threshold works for both new types, so they share it with people.
- **Organisations:** same company 0.85–0.88, a company that moved offices 0.63; parent/subsidiary, a shared address and same-name companies 0.05–0.08.
- **Products:** same product 0.88–0.97; size or colour variants, a 6-pack and same-name different brands 0.05–0.08.

**What to test**
- **Records:** upload a file and PairSight says "These look like organisations" (or people, or products) with a three-way picker; if the file is ambiguous it asks instead. Switching the type re-suggests that type's fields. Try `e2e/fixtures/company-source.csv` or `prisma/demo/sandbox-products.csv`.
- **Auto-mapping:** a much larger English dictionary per type (CRM, ERP and shop export names) plus a check of sample values. A column of emails, websites or barcodes gets mapped even with a meaningless name, and old false matches like "Hotel" → phone and "Last updated" → last name are gone. About 120 real column names are covered by tests.
- **Rules:** each type has its own presets and defaults.
  - **Organisations:** website domain, registration number and name + postcode; legal forms like "Ltd" are ignored when comparing names.
  - **Products:** barcode (UPC and EAN of the same item count as one), part number + brand and name + brand.
  - The definition of "the same" is type-specific too.
- **Results, pairs and exports:** organisations show their name, products show brand + name. Copy that said "same person" now says "judged the same".
- **Dashboard:** each evaluation shows its type.
- **Sandbox:** the banner switches between People, Organisations and Products. I recorded the two new sandboxes with real Jev runs, 13 pairs each.

**Things you should know**
- **People are unaffected:** a person evaluation sends Jev exactly the same question and uses the same judgment key as before, so existing scores and the person sandbox stay valid. A test pins this.
- **Migration:** one generated migration adds the type to evaluations and runs. Older evaluations read as people.
- **Sandbox choice is a cookie, not the URL:** a URL parameter would have broken every sandbox link and the run pages.
- **Replacing records with another kind of file** resets the rules and the definition to that type's defaults. The replace dialog now warns about this, but no e2e test covers it.
- **Not done from the plan:** hiding the country field for products, and normalising dates of birth to ISO.
- **Local leftovers:** the recordings left two `sandbox-recorder+…@pairsight.test` accounts in your local database.

To try it, restart your dev server. The migration and `pnpm demo:seed` have already run on your local database.

When you're happy, tell me and I'll run local-review and e2e-review again and commit in groups.

To try it, restart your dev server and export from any run's Results, or from the sandbox. The PDF takes the longest, so it shows the progress best.

# Prompt audit

**What I checked:** your agent instructions, skills and commands, the rule files they point to, the Claude review workflow, and the code that calls a model. Nothing was edited.

**Which model they're judged against:**
- **Instruction files, skills, commands:** Claude Opus 5.5. That's the model running this audit; nothing in the repo pins one for them.
- **App prompt (`src/lib/pairsight/entity-types.ts`, `src/server/pairsight/judgment.ts`, `src/server/ai/typesafe.ts`):** these go to TypeSafe's Jev, not Claude. I only checked them for general problems and propose nothing provider-specific.
- **`src/server/ai/anthropic.ts`:** an unused placeholder.

**Skipped:** settings, `.env*` and MCP files (they can hold secrets), and `~/.claude/extract-log.py` (outside the project).

## Summary

Your instruction files are in good shape. There's almost no shouting, no "think step by step", no retired model names, and no word caps or update suppressors. Every path and `pnpm` script they name exists.

The problems are facts the repo has outgrown since it was set up from the template:

1. **Migrations:** four template files still say "CI generates migrations". PairSight generates them locally and commits them, as `docs/rules/migrations.md` and `AGENTS.md` (both newer) say. The riskiest is `e2e-build`, which tells an agent "do NOT hand-write a migration — CI generates it". An agent following it would ship a schema change with no migration.
2. **`seed-for-pr`:** its command can't work here. `pnpm tsx` isn't installed, and a script that imports the app's `db` crashes on load outside Next.
3. **`docs/rules/authorization.md`:** it describes an admin layout that mounts `<AuthzProvider>`, but that layout was removed in `abf3f61`.

**Findings by group:**
- **Group 1, outdated prompt wording:** 1 (low, flag)
- **Group 2, stale or conflicting instruction files:** 8 (3 high with proposed edits, 5 flags)
- **Group 3, tool descriptions:** not applicable (the app gives no model any tools)
- **Group 4, request code:** 1 (low, flag)

## Findings

| # | Location | Evidence | Pattern | Why it's out of date | Confidence | Action |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `docs/rules/authorization.md:49` | "`requireAdmin()` — any active seat (the coarse gate the admin layout uses)." | G2 Volatile specifics | `src/app/[locale]/admin/layout.tsx` was deleted in `abf3f61`, and nothing in `src/` calls `requireAdmin` now. | High | `rewrite` (diff 1) |
| 2 | `docs/rules/authorization.md:80-83` | "mounts `<AuthzProvider permissions={…}>` in the **admin route-group layout**" | G2 Volatile specifics | Same layout. `<AuthzProvider>` is defined in `src/lib/rbac/authz-provider.tsx` but mounted nowhere. | High | `rewrite` (diff 2) |
| 3 | `.claude/skills/seed-for-pr/SKILL.md:17-21` | "imports `db` from `@/server/db/client`" … "`pnpm tsx scripts/seed-<name>.mjs`" | G2 Volatile specifics | `tsx` isn't a dependency. `src/server/db/client.ts:1` imports `'server-only'`, which throws in a plain Node script. The pattern that works here is `scripts/demo-seed.mts`: its own `new PrismaClient()`, run through `scripts/register-ts.mjs`. | High | `rewrite` (diff 3) |

The rules card now shows how blocking rules combine: OR between rules, AND within a rule. The full e2e suite passed 40/40, and typecheck and lint are clean. Nothing is committed.

**What changed on the Rules screen:**
- **A note under the card heading** with two short definitions:
  - **Between rules (OR):** "A pair is compared when any switched-on rule finds it. One matching rule is enough."
  - **Within a rule (AND):** "Every field in the rule must match. A pair that matches only some of them isn't found by that rule."
- **OR dividers** sit between every rule in the list, including before your own composite rules. That makes the "any one rule is enough" behaviour visible without reading the note.
- **AND chips** appear under any rule that uses more than one field. For example, "Same name and postcode" now shows `Name AND Postcode`. They're greyed out when the rule is off. Your own composite rules use the same chips instead of the old "+".

I've fixed the header showing "Running" after a run finishes, and the full e2e suite passed 40/40. Nothing is committed.

**Cause:** when a run finished with the Runs dialog open, the app did two things back to back. It asked the page to refresh, then moved you to the results page. In Next.js, moving to a new page cancels any refresh still in progress. The results page loaded fresh, but the header belongs to the shared layout around it. That layout kept the "Running" from before.

**Fix:** in `src/components/pairsight/runs/runs-centre.tsx` the refresh now happens after the move to results. Next.js runs it once the move completes, so the header updates to "Complete", or "Needs attention" if some pairs couldn't be judged. The "View results" button on the completion toast was never affected.

**Test:** the e2e step that starts a run now also checks the header after the dialog closes. It expects "Complete" or "Needs attention", not "Running". It passes with the fix. I didn't rebuild the old code to watch it fail first, so it hasn't been shown to catch the bug.

I've stopped the test servers again and cleared the e2e accounts' `ai_usage` rows. Your :3000 dev server should pick up the change, so another live run should show the header correct after the redirect.

I've tightened the type detection so a file with a plain person-name column is read as people. I couldn't reproduce "Full Name" mapping to Company Name exactly, though, so it's worth a quick look with your file. Nothing is committed.

**What I found:** "Full Name" never matches Company Name, under any type. Within one type, the mapping always uses that type's own suggestions. So the only way to get a Company Name suggestion was for the file to be detected as organisations. I found one way a people file gets there: it has an employer column whose values end in "Ltd", "Inc", "GmbH" and so on. Those endings count as strong evidence of organisations, enough to outweigh the "Full Name" column. Once the type flips, any "Name" or "Customer Name" column is mapped to Company Name.

**Fix** (`src/lib/pairsight/field-dictionary.ts`):
- A column plainly named for a person ("Full Name", "First Name", "Surname", "Date of Birth") now counts as evidence of people.
- When such a column is present, "Acme Ltd" values in another column are read as an employer, not as a sign the file is organisations.
- Names like "Contact Name" or "Customer Name" don't count, because company files often have a contact person. A company export with a "Contact Name" column is still detected as organisations.

Each of those cases now has a unit test. The dictionary tests pass, the full unit suite has the same 284 passes and the same 10 Windows-only failures, and typecheck and lint are clean. I didn't rerun e2e this time: the company and product test files have no person-name columns, so their detection doesn't change.

If you still have the file that misbehaved, could you send its column headers (just the names, no data)? If it didn't have a company-style column, this fix won't cover it, and the headers will show me what did.

Here's everything since the last devlog list (2 Oct, 20:43). That list ended with exports and Excel import on `feat/polish`, uncommitted while you tested. This picks up from there.

## 3 Oct: PDF summary report and PR #8 (merged)
- **Summary report** in the Export menu, built for an analyst who has to justify a vendor choice to their boss:
  - Which vendor matched most like Jev, and whether that holds across thresholds.
  - What each vendor gets wrong.
  - A head to head between the vendors ("Jev sided with X in N of M").
  - Example pairs with names and key fields.
  - The method and scope behind the numbers.
  - It's drawn on the server with `@react-pdf/renderer`. It works in the sandbox signed out, and someone else's run gets a 404.
- **Page 3 layout fix:** cards that weren't allowed to split across pages were nested inside each other, which broke the layout. They now sit directly on the page.
- The branch was renamed from `feat/polish` to `feat/import-export-and-polish`. Local review and full e2e passed, then PR #8 was opened ("CSV and Excel import/export, a PDF summary report, landing and Glass polish"). You merged it.

## 3 Oct: organisations and products (`feat/entity-types-and-field-dictionary`)
- **Exploration finding:** Jev's API has no notion of record type. It's always "two records plus one yes/no question", so everything person-specific was PairSight's own code.
- **Three record types:** people, organisations and products, with one registry (`entity-types.ts`) holding what differs per type:
  - **Fields:** 27 in all.
    - People: name, email, phone, date of birth, address.
    - Organisations: company and trading name, registration number, tax ID, website, industry.
    - Products: name, brand, manufacturer, GTIN, MPN, SKU, model, category, colour, size.
  - **Blocking presets and defaults per type:**
    - Organisations: website domain, registration number, name + postcode.
    - Products: GTIN, MPN + brand, name + brand.
  - **The Jev question and its criteria per type.** Parent vs. subsidiary counts as different organisations; size or colour variants and multipacks count as different products.
  - **New normalisers:**
    - Company names lose legal suffixes like Ltd, GmbH and Inc.
    - Websites reduce to the domain.
    - GTINs are validated by check digit.
    - Codes are uppercased with punctuation removed.
  - **People unchanged:** the person flow is unchanged, so old judgments and the sandbox recording stay valid.
- **Database:** an `EntityType` enum, `Evaluation.entityType` and `Run.entityType` (existing runs default to person), with a generated migration.
- **Type detected from the upload:** a "These look like…" picker on Records, which asks when unsure. Switching type re-suggests the mapping without another upload. Replacing a file with a different type resets that evaluation's rules and identity text.
- **Much bigger field dictionary** (`field-synonyms.ts` for the data, `field-dictionary.ts` for the logic):
  - It covers column names from common CRM and ERP exports (Salesforce, HubSpot, Dynamics, SAP, Shopify).
  - Matches are by whole word only, which ended the old false hits like "hotel" → phone and "ethnicity" → city.
  - It understands qualifiers, so "Billing Postcode" → postcode.
  - Sample values confirm or overrule a guess: emails, phones, dates, websites, valid GTINs. A column named "col7" full of emails gets suggested as email.
  - It has its own unit tests.
- **Live Jev check** on fictional pairs, recorded in V1 Scope:
  - Organisations: same 0.85–0.88 (a moved office 0.63), different 0.05–0.08.
  - Products: same 0.88–0.97, different 0.05–0.08.
  - 0.50 holds for every type.
- **Stub and scripts:** the Jev stub scores each type, and `jev-check` and `demo-seed` handle all three.

## 3 Oct: changes from your testing (same branch)
- **Export progress:** an export shows a "Preparing…" toast while the file is generated, then "Download started" or an error. The button shows a spinner meanwhile.
- **Sandbox as three evaluations:** you asked for evaluations instead of a type switch. The sandbox dashboard now lists Customer master, Supplier master and Product catalogue side by side, each opening at `/sandbox/[id]/…`. All three use real recorded Jev runs: organisations 13 pairs (8 the same), products 13 pairs (5 the same).
- **Prompt audit** (`/claude-api prompt-audit`): report and proposed diff delivered, not applied. Main findings:
  - Stale admin-layout references in the authorization rules.
  - The `seed-for-pr` skill calls `pnpm tsx`, which doesn't exist.
  - Several "CI generates migrations" lines that contradict local generation.
  - The cicd command still asks for screenshots, against your no-screenshots preference.
- **AND/OR on blocking rules:**
  - A blue **OR** tag on dashed dividers between rules.
  - Amber **AND** tags between the fields of a multi-field rule.
  - Both are in JetBrains Mono.
  - A "How rules combine" hover explainer replaces the inline box.
  - Rule labels everywhere, including exports and the PDF, now read "Email AND Postcode".
- **Bug fix, "Running" after a finished run:** with the Runs dialog open, moving to Results cancelled the header's refresh, so it kept saying "Running". The refresh now happens after the move, and an e2e check covers it.
- **Bug fix, a people file read as organisations:** an employer column with "Acme Ltd"-style values outweighed "Full Name". Now a plainly person-named column ("Full Name", "Surname", "DOB") means people. "Contact Name" still doesn't flip a company file.

## 3 Oct: review and commits
- `/local-review`:
  - All gates passed, including the RLS check (15 tables) and the production build.
  - `check:standards` meets 17 of 24 and 10 unit tests fail; both come from Windows (no exec bit, and the script can't launch `eslint`/`tsc`), not this branch.
  - The manual pass found nothing.
- `/e2e-review`: 40/40 passing.
- 7 commits, not pushed:
  - records support for organisations and products
  - three sandbox evaluations
  - export progress
  - AND/OR rules
  - the top bar fix
  - e2e coverage
  - docs
- The session log stays uncommitted until the push.
